\doxysubsection{Detailed Description}
The bigint implementation as used by the ax\+TLS project. 

The bigint library is for RSA encryption/decryption as well as signing. This code tries to minimise use of malloc/free by maintaining a small cache. A bigint context may maintain state by being made "{}permanent"{}. It be be later released with a \doxylink{group__bigint__api_gacbe10d3a9d7b19085091b23f5c64f67a}{bi\+\_\+depermanent()} and \doxylink{group__bigint__api_ga8595bd77c64878c62483f4c0db247e7f}{bi\+\_\+free()} call.

It supports the following reduction techniques\+:
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item Classical
\item Barrett
\item Montgomery
\end{DoxyItemize}

It also implements the following\+:
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item Karatsuba multiplication
\item Squaring
\item Sliding window exponentiation
\item Chinese Remainder Theorem (implemented in rsa.\+c).
\end{DoxyItemize}

All the algorithms used are pretty standard, and designed for different data bus sizes. Negative numbers are not dealt with at all, so a subtraction may need to be tested for negativity.

This library steals some ideas from Jef Poskanzer \href{http://cs.marlboro.edu/term/cs-fall02/algorithms/crypto/RSA/bigint}{\texttt{http\+://cs.\+marlboro.\+edu/term/cs-\/fall02/algorithms/crypto/\+RSA/bigint}} and GMP \href{http://www.swox.com/gmp}{\texttt{http\+://www.\+swox.\+com/gmp}}. It gets most of its implementation detail from "{}\+The Handbook of Applied Cryptography"{} \href{http://www.cacr.math.uwaterloo.ca/hac/about/chap14.pdf}{\texttt{http\+://www.\+cacr.\+math.\+uwaterloo.\+ca/hac/about/chap14.\+pdf}}

Pre-\/calculate some of the expensive steps in reduction. 

This function should only be called once (normally when a session starts). When the session is over, \doxylink{group__bigint__api_gaa86cfcf8c5515af4b420ab56e3d4e266}{bi\+\_\+free\+\_\+mod()} should be called. \doxylink{group__bigint__api_ga900c174a99b22f6fe08aba64edfc6b3e}{bi\+\_\+mod\+\_\+power()} relies on this function being called. 
\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em ctx} & \mbox{[}in\mbox{]} The bigint session context. \\
\hline
{\em bim} & \mbox{[}in\mbox{]} The bigint modulus that will be used. \\
\hline
{\em mod\+\_\+offset} & \mbox{[}in\mbox{]} There are three moduluii that can be stored -\/ the standard modulus, and its two primes p and q. This offset refers to which modulus we are referring to. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}

\mbox{\hyperlink{mongoose_8c_abe581277bc5d79aef6f1310049fa2411}{bigint}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} regular\+\_\+multiply (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{mongoose_8c_a91376585a24c60b48824a692db9e5e13}{BI\+\_\+\+CTX}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{ctx}{, }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{mongoose_8c_abe581277bc5d79aef6f1310049fa2411}{bigint}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{bia}{, }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{mongoose_8c_abe581277bc5d79aef6f1310049fa2411}{bigint}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{bib}{, }\item[{int}]{inner\+\_\+partial}{, }\item[{int}]{outer\+\_\+partial}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

Perform a standard multiplication between two bigints.

Barrett reduction has no need for some parts of the product, so ignore bits of the multiply. This routine gives Barrett its big performance improvements over Classical/\+Montgomery reduction methods. \Hypertarget{group__bigint__api_gaecb4eaf87a8ad152658f15709d788bfe}\index{Big Integer API@{Big Integer API}!regularize\_k@{regularize\_k}}
